        "text": "The AISecOps Specialist (The Warden) is a brainstorming and specification skill for the \"LLM as Database\" agent harness framework.         "text": "The Framework Council is a multi-specialist brainstorming and specification skill for the \"LLM as Database\" agent harness framework.       {
        "member": "orchestration-comparativist/SKILL.md",
        "line": 4,
        "text": "The Orchestration Comparativist is a brainstorming and specification skill for the \"LLM as Database\" agent harness framework. Activate this skill whenever the user is evaluating, comparing, or selecting an orchestration approach for their multi-agent harness — including Pi, n8n, a Claude agent harness, OpenClaw, LangGraph, or any custom orchestration layer. Also triggers when the user asks what an orchestrator needs to be able to do given the requirements surfaced by other spec skills, when comparing automation platforms for multi-agent coordination, or when the orchestration decision has been left open and needs structured evaluation. This skill does not advocate for any specific orchestrator. It maps harness requirements against orchestration capabilities, surfaces tradeoffs, and helps the user arrive at a deliberate, documented decision. It does not produce implementation code — it produces requirement-to-capability mappings, comparison frameworks, and structured decision rationale."
      },
      {
        "member": "orchestration-comparativist/SKILL.md",
        "line": 28,
        "text": "- What write operations need to be sequenced, and can any happen concurrently? (MEMIT Specialist)"
      },
      {
        "member": "orchestration-comparativist/SKILL.md",
        "line": 41,
        "text": "- If an OQ's resolution depends on a design decision in another domain (e.g., a MEMIT sequencing constraint that affects scheduling, or a Warden authorization rule that affects HIL gate design): flag it as a cross-domain dependency and note which session summary owns the input."
      },
      {
        "member": "orchestration-comparativist/SKILL.md",
        "line": 84,
        "text": "- Triggering parametric memory operations (MEMIT compile) as first-class workflow steps"
      },
      {
        "member": "orchestration-comparativist/SKILL.md",
        "line": 105,
        "text": "Can the orchestrator treat a MEMIT compile operation, a Git commit, a PyTest run, and a language model inference call as equivalent first-class steps?"
      },
      {
        "member": "orchestration-comparativist/SKILL.md",
        "line": 107,
        "text": "Most workflow tools model steps as HTTP calls or function invocations. MEMIT compile is a GPU-bound operation with variable latency and resource constraints. The orchestrator must handle this without treating it as a black box timeout."
      },
      {
        "member": "orchestration-comparativist/SKILL.md",
        "line": 110,
        "text": "Can the orchestrator implement the prepare → commit → compensate pattern across two independent subsystems (Git + MEMIT) with automatic rollback on partial failure?"
      },
      {
        "member": "orchestration-comparativist/SKILL.md",
        "line": 123,
        "text": "Can the orchestrator safely manage concurrent agent tasks that may generate competing `.larql` patches? Does it serialize writes automatically or require explicit locking?"
      },
      {
        "member": "orchestration-comparativist/SKILL.md",
        "line": 168,
        "text": "**Risk:** The Reflexion loop with accumulating verbal context is unnatural in n8n's execution model — each run is relatively stateless. MEMIT operations require wrapping in an HTTP service to be addressable as nodes."
      },
      {
        "member": "orchestration-comparativist/SKILL.md",
        "line": 267,
        "text": "- Is the schema extension workflow specced? If an agent produces a `.larql` patch that implies a new entity type or relation not in the current schema, what is the orchestration event? Who is notified? Who authorizes? Through which gate? If this workflow is unspecced, name it as a **required orchestration event** that must be defined before the evaluation matrix is final."
      },
      {
        "member": "orchestration-comparativist/SKILL.md",
        "line": 283,
        "text": "3. **The MEMIT step as a black box** — Most workflow tools model steps as fast, stateless HTTP calls. MEMIT compile is GPU-bound with variable latency. An orchestrator that can't handle slow, resource-intensive steps will time out or deadlock on this operation."
      },
      {
        "member": "orchestration-comparativist/SKILL.md",
        "line": 312,
        "text": "- [ ] MEMIT compile step explicitly addressed in evaluation (slow, GPU-bound)"
      }
    ]

      {
        "member": "state-consistency-theorist/SKILL.md",
        "line": 4,
        "text": "The State Consistency Theorist is a brainstorming and specification skill for the \"LLM as Database\" agent harness framework. Activate this skill whenever the user is reasoning about keeping two memory mediums in sync — the code repository (Git) and the parametric memory layer (.vindex overlay). Also triggers when discussing the two-phase commit pattern, the State Ledger (mapping Git commit hashes to .vindex snapshot hashes), the Transaction Controller and circuit breaker behavior, temporal reconciliation, the Pruning Agent and semantic garbage collection, rollback coordination across both mediums, or what \"consistent state\" means when the system has both a file system and a neural knowledge graph. This skill is orchestration-agnostic — it does not advocate for Pi, n8n, Claude harness, or any specific orchestrator. It produces consistency guarantees, failure mode specifications, and transaction boundary definitions, not implementation code."
      },
      {
        "member": "state-consistency-theorist/SKILL.md",
        "line": 14,
        "text": "You do not own the write engine (MEMIT Specialist). You do not own the trust boundary (Warden). You do not own the correctness of what gets written (Validation Contract Architect). You own the **consistency contract between the two state mediums** and the **behavior of the system when that contract is violated.**"
      },
      {
        "member": "state-consistency-theorist/SKILL.md",
        "line": 193,
        "text": "└── Queryable: SELECT * FROM EDGES (larql)"
      },
      {
        "member": "state-consistency-theorist/SKILL.md",
        "line": 194,
        "text": "└── Write operation: MEMIT compile"
      },
      {
        "member": "state-consistency-theorist/SKILL.md",
        "line": 209,
        "text": "2. Compile the `.larql` patch to the `.vindex` (overlay written, not yet mounted as active)"
      },
      {
        "member": "state-consistency-theorist/SKILL.md",
        "line": 220,
        "text": "**The failure case:** Phase 2 can still fail after a successful Phase 1. The Git commit pushes successfully, but the `.vindex` mount fails (e.g., GPU memory exhaustion during MEMIT compile). This is the divergence scenario the Transaction Controller exists to handle."
      },
      {
        "member": "state-consistency-theorist/SKILL.md",
        "line": 306,
        "text": "- A Git commit is made without a corresponding `.larql` patch (code changed, no knowledge update needed — this is valid and must be handled gracefully)"
      },
      {
        "member": "state-consistency-theorist/SKILL.md",
        "line": 307,
        "text": "- A `.larql` patch is compiled without a corresponding Git commit (knowledge updated, no code change — this is a valid scenario for domain fact updates, but must be logged)"
      },
      {
        "member": "state-consistency-theorist/SKILL.md",
        "line": 325,
        "text": "3. Generate a `.larql` batch of `DELETE FROM EDGES` commands"
      },
      {
        "member": "state-consistency-theorist/SKILL.md",
        "line": 355,
        "text": "4. **Concurrent write assumption** — If multiple agents can generate `.larql` patches simultaneously, the two-phase commit must handle merge conflicts. Many systems assume serialized writes in the spec and discover concurrency issues in production."
      },
      {
        "member": "state-consistency-theorist/SKILL.md",
        "line": 416,
        "text": "- [ ] Interface contracts with MEMIT Specialist, Warden, and Validation layer defined"
      }
    ]
  },

  {
    "path": "validation-contract-architect.skill",
    "members": [
      {
        "member": "validation-contract-architect/SKILL.md",
        "bytes": 21644
      }
    ],
    "program_hits": [
      {
        "member": "validation-contract-architect/SKILL.md",
        "line": 4,
        "text": "The Validation Contract Architect is a brainstorming and specification skill for the \"LLM as Database\" agent harness framework. Activate this skill whenever the user is reasoning about what makes a proposed write valid before it reaches the commit stage, the deterministic sandbox, the Reflexion loop (Coder → Test → Fail → Fix → Retry), PyTest harness requirements, the Actor-Critic or Generator-Evaluator pattern, what \"passing validation\" means for code outputs and .larql patches, or how the system decides a Coder Agent's work is trustworthy enough to proceed to the write engine. Also triggers when discussing retry exit criteria, unrecoverable failures, or how the Validator Agent is itself kept honest. This skill does not produce implementation code — it produces correctness contracts, sandbox specifications, and validation pipeline definitions. Orchestration-agnostic; distinct from security concerns (owned by the Warden) and consistency concerns (owned by the State Consistency Theorist)."
      },
      {
        "member": "validation-contract-architect/SKILL.md",
        "line": 14,
        "text": "2. **A `.larql` patch** — proposed edges to write into the parametric memory layer"
      },
      {
        "member": "validation-contract-architect/SKILL.md",
        "line": 29,
        "text": "- Should the Validator Agent be able to reject a `.larql` patch independently of the code — or only as a pair?"
      },
      {
        "member": "validation-contract-architect/SKILL.md",
        "line": 144,
        "text": "2. Compiles incorrect facts into the model's parametric memory via MEMIT (expensive to reverse, potentially invisible)"
      },
      {
        "member": "validation-contract-architect/SKILL.md",
        "line": 157,
        "text": "↓  produces (code + .larql patch)"
      },
      {
        "member": "validation-contract-architect/SKILL.md",
        "line": 182,
        "text": "- **Actor (Coder Agent):** Produces outputs — code and `.larql` patches"
      },
      {
        "member": "validation-contract-architect/SKILL.md",
        "line": 208,
        "text": "- Validating the `.larql` patch independently of the code behavior"
      },
      {
        "member": "validation-contract-architect/SKILL.md",
        "line": 211,
        "text": "### Validating the `.larql` Patch"
      },
      {
        "member": "validation-contract-architect/SKILL.md",
        "line": 213,
        "text": "Code validation via PyTest is well-understood. Validating a `.larql` patch is less obvious and requires explicit spec attention."
      },
      {
        "member": "validation-contract-architect/SKILL.md",
        "line": 215,
        "text": "A `.larql` patch is valid if:"
      },
      {
        "member": "validation-contract-architect/SKILL.md",
        "line": 236,
        "text": "- `.larql` patch passes all four validity checks"
      },
      {
        "member": "validation-contract-architect/SKILL.md",
        "line": 274,
        "text": "4. **`.larql` patch validation as afterthought** — Most architects spec code validation rigorously and treat the patch as secondary. But an incorrect patch that passes alongside correct code is the more dangerous failure — it silently corrupts the knowledge graph."
      },
      {
        "member": "validation-contract-architect/SKILL.md",
        "line": 286,
        "text": "- *\"If the Coder Agent passes all its tests but the `.larql` patch claims a dependency that doesn't exist in the code — which layer catches that?\"*"
      },
      {
        "member": "validation-contract-architect/SKILL.md",
        "line": 302,
        "text": "- [ ] `.larql` patch validation criteria defined (syntactic, schema, behavioral, scope)"
      },
      {
        "member": "validation-contract-architect/SKILL.md",
        "line": 303,
        "text": "- [ ] `.larql` schema version header requirement resolved: does the patch declare a version field, and does the L1 static check verify it matches the active spec version? If schema versioning is undecided, flag as OQ referencing GAP-3 / OQ-W6 — but the slot must still appear in the L1 checklist"
      },
      {
        "member": "validation-contract-architect/SKILL.md",
        "line": 311,
        "text": "- [ ] Interface contracts with MEMIT Specialist, Warden, State Consistency Theorist, and Orchestration layer defined"
      }
    ]
  }
